<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove integral Donovan finiteness: for every prime <i>p</i> and positive integer <i>M</i>, the blocks of all finite groups with defect groups of order at most <i>M</i> have only finitely many Morita equivalence classes over $`W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)`$. The defect groups need not be abelian, and the result includes <i>p</i> = 2. The same bounded-defect finiteness holds over each fixed complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero with algebraically closed residue field of characteristic <i>p</i>, including ramified rings.

- **claim-integral-donovan** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $p$ be a prime, let $\mathcal O=W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)$, and let $M\geq1$ be an integer. As $G$ ranges over all finite groups and $b$ ranges over all blocks of $\mathcal OG$ whose defect groups have order at most $M$, the algebras $\mathcal OG b$ belong to only finitely many $\mathcal O$-linear Morita equivalence classes.
  - Explanation: Bounding the defect order gives a finite collection of integral Morita types over the Witt ring, while the ambient finite group may vary without an order bound.
- **claim-fixed-complete-coefficients** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-complete-dvr)): Let $p$ be a prime, let $\mathcal R$ be a fixed complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero whose residue field $\ell$ is algebraically closed of characteristic $p$, and let $M\geq1$ be an integer. As $G$ ranges over all finite groups and $b$ ranges over primitive central idempotents of $\mathcal R G$ for which the residue block $\ell G\bar b$ has a defect group of order at most $M$, the algebras $\mathcal R G b$ belong to only finitely many $\mathcal R$-linear Morita equivalence classes.
  - Explanation: The finiteness assertion extends to each fixed complete coefficient ring with the stated characteristic and algebraically closed residue field, including ramified rings.
- **claim-cartan-and-frobenius-criteria** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-criteria)): The following assertions will be used.* 1. *For every $p$ and $M$, the Cartan sums of all finite-group blocks over $\mathcal O$ with defect order at most $M$ are bounded by a constant $c(p,M)$.* 2. *For fixed positive bounds on the defect order, Cartan sum, and integral Morita–Frobenius number, finite-group blocks over $\mathcal O$ have only finitely many $\mathcal O$-linear Morita equivalence classes.
  - Explanation: The Cartan bound comes from field Morita finiteness. The integral criterion additionally requires bounded defect and integral Morita–Frobenius number.
- **claim-controlled-integral-extensions** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-structure)): Let $(H,B)$ be a reduced pair with defect order at most $M$. Put $N=F^*(H)$, $X=H/N$, and let $b$ be the block of $\mathcal ON$ covered by $B$. Then the following constructions and bounds hold.* 1. *The group $N$ is a central product $$N=PZK_1\cdots K_j,\qquad P=O_p(H),\quad Z=O_{p'}(H)\leq Z(H),$$ where the $K_i$ are quasisimple components, $\|P\|$ and $j$ are bounded, and $[X:O_{p'}(X)]$ is bounded. The latter index is bounded also for every subgroup of every extension of $X$ by a $p'$-group.* 2. *There is an extension $N\trianglelefteq\bar N$ with abelian $p'$-quotient $A_0=\bar N/N$. For representatives $h_x$ of $x\in X$, normalized by $h_1=1$, write $h_xh_y=n_{x,y}h_{xy}$ with $n_{x,y}\in N$. Conjugation extends to automorphisms $\alpha_x$ of $\bar N$ satisfying the actual identities $$\begin{aligned} \alpha_x\alpha_y&=\operatorname{ad}(n_{x,y})\alpha_{xy}, \label{eq:actual-actions}\\ n_{x,y}n_{xy,z}&=\alpha_x(n_{y,z})n_{x,yz}. \label{eq:actual-factors} \end{aligned}$$ Every block $\bar b$ of $\mathcal O\bar N$ covering $b$ has bounded defect.* 3. *Put $\Xi=\operatorname{Hom}(A_0,k^\times)$, using Teichmüller lifts of the characters over $\mathcal O$, and $Y=\Xi\rtimes X$. There is a $Y$-crossed order with identity component $\mathcal O\bar N$ in which $\mathcal OH$ is a full idempotent corner. After taking an appropriate central summand and a further full corner, this is a crossed order with identity component $$B_0=\mathcal O\bar N\bar b$$ and grading group $Q=\operatorname{Stab}_Y(\bar b)$. In the pure $X$-degrees the actions and factors are those in (ii). The index $[Q:O_{p'}(Q)]$ is bounded.* 4. *The basic orders of all these identity blocks $B_0$ belong to a finite list of integral orders $R_1,\ldots,R_t$. Their integral Picard groups, and in particular their outer automorphism groups, are finite.
  - Explanation: A reduced pair admits crossed-order realizations retaining the actual group factors, with bounded quotient index and a finite list of integral basic identity orders.
- **claim-integral-orbit-finiteness** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-orbit)): Let $J$ be a smooth affine group scheme of finite type over $\mathcal O$, acting on an affine scheme $V$ of finite type over $\mathcal O$. For $x\in V(\mathcal O)$ let $T_xV$ denote the integral tangent module along the section $x$. The points for which the orbit derivative $$\operatorname{Lie}(J)\longrightarrow T_xV$$ is surjective belong to only finitely many $J(\mathcal O)$-orbits.
  - Explanation: Surjectivity of the integral orbit derivative gives finitely many integral orbits through a special-fibre orbit argument and fixed Hensel slices.
- **claim-hochschild-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-hh)): Let $A$ be an $\mathcal O$-order Morita equivalent to a finite direct sum of finite-group block orders. Then $\operatorname{HH}^1_{\mathcal O}(A)=0$, and $\operatorname{Out}_{\mathcal O}(A)$ is finite.
  - Explanation: Orders in the specified block Morita classes have no outer derivations and have finite outer automorphism groups.
- **claim-fixed-total-labelled-gradings** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-gradings)): Let $A$ be an $\mathcal O$-order with $\operatorname{HH}^1_{\mathcal O}(A)=0$, and let $H$ be any fixed finite group. There are only finitely many $H$-labelled gradings of $A$ up to $\mathcal O$-algebra automorphism. In fact there are only finitely many orbits under inner automorphisms.
  - Explanation: A fixed total order with vanishing first Hochschild cohomology admits finitely many labelled gradings even up to inner automorphism; the grading group need not have order prime to the residue characteristic.
- **claim-retaining-identity-markings** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-prop-marked)): Fix a basic block order $R$ and a finite group $H$. Let $\mathcal A$ be a collection of $H$-crossed orders on $R$, each Morita equivalent to a finite-group block. If the total orders in $\mathcal A$ have finitely many $\mathcal O$-Morita classes, then $\mathcal A$ has finitely many based graded isomorphism classes.
  - Explanation: For fixed identity order and grading group, finite total Morita types yield finite based graded types by bounding projective multiplicities and then controlling gradings and markings.
- **claim-teichmuller-cohomology-vanishing** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-teich-cohomology)): If $S$ is a finite $p$-group acting trivially on $\mathcal T$, then $H^i(S,\mathcal T)=0$ for every $i>0$.
  - Explanation: The group order annihilates positive cohomology but acts invertibly on the Teichmüller coefficients, forcing that cohomology to vanish.
- **claim-exact-integral-comparison** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-comparison)): Fix $p,M$ and the data of Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">5</a>. Let $S\leq Q$ be a $p$-subgroup contained in the pure subgroup $X\leq Y$, so that $S$ stabilizes $B_0=\mathcal O\bar N\bar b$. Put $a_{s,t}=n_{s,t}\bar b$. There is a positive integer $m$, bounded in terms of $p,M$, and a $(\sigma^m B_0,B_0)$-Morita bimodule $\mathcal M$ with invertible $\mathcal O$-linear maps $J_s:\mathcal M\to\mathcal M$, normalized by $J_1=\mathrm{id}$, such that $$\begin{aligned} J_s(avb)&=\alpha'_s(a)J_s(v)\alpha_s(b), \label{eq:comparison-covariance}\\ J_sJ_t(v)&=a'_{s,t}J_{st}(v)a_{s,t}^{-1}. \label{eq:comparison-exact} \end{aligned}$$ Here $a\in\sigma^m B_0$, $b\in B_0$, $\alpha'_s=\sigma^m(\alpha_s)$, and $a'_{s,t}=\sigma^m(a_{s,t})$. The assertion includes $S=1$.
  - Explanation: The prescribed pure subgroup admits an integral Morita comparison with bounded coefficient-Frobenius return, retaining both covariance and the exact multiplication factors.
- **claim-extending-comparison-bimodules** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-induced)): Let $B,B'$ be $\mathcal O$-orders with normalized crossed systems $(\alpha_s,a_{s,t})$ and $(\alpha'_s,a'_{s,t})$ for a finite group $S$, and let $A,A'$ be their crossed orders. Suppose an $(B',B)$-Morita bimodule $\mathcal M$ has invertible $\mathcal O$-linear maps $J_s$ satisfying Equations <a href="#eq:comparison-covariance" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison-covariance">[eq:comparison-covariance]</a> and <a href="#eq:comparison-exact" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:comparison-exact">[eq:comparison-exact]</a>. Then $A$ and $A'$ are $\mathcal O$-linearly Morita equivalent.
  - Explanation: Exact covariance and factor identities extend the identity-component Morita bimodule to the crossed orders through an induced projective generator.
- **claim-actual-sylow-extension-blocks** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-sylow-block)): For the data of Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">5</a>, let $S\leq Q\cap X$ be a pure $p$-subgroup. The restriction of the crossed order to $S$, with identity component $B_0$, is a block of a finite group. Its defect order is bounded in terms of $p,M$, and its integral Morita–Frobenius number is bounded in the same parameters. All these restriction blocks have finitely many integral Morita classes.
  - Explanation: A pure subgroup restriction is an actual finite-group block. Its bounded defect and comparison period permit the numerical integral finiteness criterion.
- **claim-based-subgroup-finiteness** (classification, [source](#stmt-thm-based-sylow)): Compress the crossed orders of Proposition <a href="#prop:structure" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:structure">5</a> by a basic idempotent in the identity block, and identify the resulting identity order with a representative $R_i$ of its finite list. For every possible abstract $p$-group $S$, their restrictions to $S$ have finitely many based graded isomorphism classes. The identity-order identifications may be chosen arbitrarily over $\mathcal O$.
  - Explanation: Basic compression and rigidity turn the finite Morita lists of restriction blocks into finite lists preserving subgroup labels and the chosen identity-order identification.
- **claim-central-unit-decomposition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-center-units)): Let $R$ be an indecomposable $\mathcal O$-order and put $Z_R=Z(R)$. Then $Z_R$ is local with residue field $k$, and $$\label{eq:center-units} Z_R^\times=\mathcal T\times U_R, \qquad U_R=1+\operatorname{rad}Z_R.$$ This decomposition is invariant under every $\mathcal O$-algebra automorphism of $R$. If $n$ is prime to $p$, the map $u\mapsto u^n$ is an automorphism of $U_R$.
  - Explanation: The centre is local, and its units split canonically into Teichmüller and principal units. Powers prime to the residue characteristic act invertibly on the principal units.
- **claim-finite-sylow-restriction-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-restriction)): Let a finite group $Q$ act by $\mathcal O$-algebra automorphisms on $Z_R$, and let $S$ be a Sylow $p$-subgroup of $Q$. Then $$\operatorname{res}:H^2(Q,Z_R^\times)\longrightarrow H^2(S,Z_R^\times)$$ has finite kernel.
  - Explanation: Sylow restriction is injective on principal-unit cohomology, while the full Teichmüller cohomology contribution is finite.
- **claim-integral-kernel-removal** (conditional_result, [source](#stmt-thm-kernel)): Fix an indecomposable basic $\mathcal O$-order $R$ Morita equivalent to a finite-group block, and a finite subgroup $\mathcal F\leq\operatorname{Out}_{\mathcal O}(R)$. Consider crossed orders on $R$ by finite groups $Q$ with the following properties:* 1. *the indices $[Q:O_{p'}(Q)]$ are bounded;* 2. *every outer action has image in $\mathcal F$;* 3. *for each possible abstract $p$-group, the restrictions to subgroups of that type have finitely many based graded isomorphism classes.* *Then the block summands of these crossed orders have only finitely many $\mathcal O$-linear Morita equivalence classes.
  - Explanation: Matrix corners remove the unbounded normal subgroup of order prime to the residue characteristic. Determinant normalization preserves the based Sylow restrictions, and finite restriction fibres finish the classification.
- **claim-integral-lifts-of-field-basic-orders** (theorem, [source](#stmt-cor-reduction)): For every $p,M$, there is a finite list of integral basic orders $R_1',\ldots,R_v'$ such that the basic algebra of every block of $kG$ of defect order at most $M$ is isomorphic to $k\otimes_{\mathcal O}R_i'$ for some $i$. In particular the blocks over $k$ of bounded defect have finitely many $k$-linear Morita classes.
  - Explanation: The finite integral basic-order list reduces to representatives of all field basic algebras with bounded defect; this is a consequence of the integral theorem.

        "anchor": "stmt-prop-structure",
        "exact_quote": "Let $(H,B)$ be a reduced pair with defect order at most $M$. Put $N=F^*(H)$, $X=H/N$, and let $b$ be the block of $\\mathcal ON$ covered by $B$. Then the following constructions and bounds hold.*\n\n1.  *The group $N$ is a central product $$N=PZK_1\\cdots K_j,\\qquad P=O_p(H),\\quad Z=O_{p'}(H)\\leq Z(H),$$ where the $K_i$ are quasisimple components, $|P|$ and $j$ are bounded, and $[X:O_{p'}(X)]$ is bounded. The latter index is bounded also for every subgroup of every extension of $X$ by a $p'$-group.*\n\n\n\n2.  *There is an extension $N\\trianglelefteq\\bar N$ with abelian $p'$-quotient $A_0=\\bar N/N$. For representatives $h_x$ of $x\\in X$, normalized by $h_1=1$, write $h_xh_y=n_{x,y}h_{xy}$ with $n_{x,y}\\in N$. Conjugation extends to automorphisms $\\alpha_x$ of $\\bar N$ satisfying the actual identities $$\\begin{aligned}\n     \\alpha_x\\alpha_y&=\\operatorname{ad}(n_{x,y})\\alpha_{xy},\n           \\label{eq:actual-actions}\\\\\n     n_{x,y}n_{xy,z}&=\\alpha_x(n_{y,z})n_{x,yz}.\n           \\label{eq:actual-factors}\n    \\end{aligned}$$ Every block $\\bar b$ of $\\mathcal O\\bar N$ covering $b$ has bounded defect.*\n\n3.  *Put $\\Xi=\\operatorname{Hom}(A_0,k^\\times)$, using Teichmüller lifts of the characters over $\\mathcal O$, and $Y=\\Xi\\rtimes X$. There is a $Y$-crossed order with identity component $\\mathcal O\\bar N$ in which $\\mathcal OH$ is a full idempotent corner. After taking an appropriate central summand and a further full corner, this is a crossed order with identity component $$B_0=\\mathcal O\\bar N\\bar b$$ and grading group $Q=\\operatorname{Stab}_Y(\\bar b)$. In the pure $X$-degrees the actions and factors are those in (ii). The index $[Q:O_{p'}(Q)]$ is bounded.*\n\n4.  *The basic orders of all these identity blocks $B_0$ belong to a finite list of integral orders $R_1,\\ldots,R_t$. Their integral Picard groups, and in particular their outer automorphism groups, are finite.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Integral block** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A block of a finite group $G$ over $\mathcal O$ is an algebra $\mathcal OG b$, where $b$ is a primitive central idempotent.
- **Coefficient-linear Morita equivalence** ([source](#sec:introduction)): All Morita equivalences in this paper are equivalences of categories of finitely generated modules which are linear over the specified coefficient ring.
- **Integral order** ([source](#pm-h-7)): An $\mathcal O$-order means a unital $\mathcal O$-algebra free of finite rank as an $\mathcal O$-module.
- **Block summand of an order** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For a general order, a block summand means its direct factor at a primitive central idempotent.
- **Coefficient-Frobenius twist** ([source](#pm-h-7)): For an order $A$, the coefficient twist $\sigma^m A$ is the order obtained by applying $\sigma^m$ to its structure constants; equivalently it is scalar transport along $\sigma^m$.
- **Integral Morita–Frobenius number** ([source](#pm-h-7)): We write $\operatorname{mf}_{\mathcal O}(A)$ for the least positive $m$ for which $A$ and $\sigma^m A$ are $\mathcal O$-linearly Morita equivalent, whenever such an $m$ exists.
- **Basic idempotent** ([source](#pm-h-7)): A basic idempotent of a block order is a sum of one primitive idempotent for every isomorphism type of indecomposable projective.
- **Normalized crossed systems, crossed orders and based isomorphisms** ([source](#stmt-auto-definition-1)): Let $R$ be an $\mathcal O$-order and $Q$ a finite group. A normalized crossed system consists of $\alpha_q\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathcal O}(R)$ and $a_{q,t}\in R^\times$ such that $$\begin{aligned} \alpha_q\alpha_t&=\operatorname{ad}(a_{q,t})\alpha_{qt}, \label{eq:action-law}\\ a_{q,t}a_{qt,v}&=\alpha_q(a_{t,v})a_{q,tv}, \label{eq:factor-law} \end{aligned}$$ with $\alpha_1=\mathrm{id}$ and $a_{1,q}=a_{q,1}=1$. Its crossed order is $A=\bigoplus_{q\in Q}Ru_q$, with $$u_qr=\alpha_q(r)u_q,\qquad u_qu_t=a_{q,t}u_{qt},\qquad u_1=1.$$ A $Q$-labelled graded isomorphism preserves each homogeneous component with its label. When the identity component is identified with a fixed $R$, such an isomorphism is *based* if it is the identity on $R$.
- **Outer action** ([source](#pm-h-8)): The induced homomorphism $Q\to\operatorname{Out}_{\mathcal O}(R)$ is the outer action.
- **Restriction of a crossed order** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For $S\leq Q$ the *restriction to $S$* is the crossed suborder $A_S=\bigoplus_{s\in S}Ru_s$, with the same identity marking and the restricted factors.
- **Reduced pair** ([source](#pm-h-9)): We call a pair $(H,B)$ reduced if $B$ is a block of $\mathcal OH$ such that every block of a normal subgroup covered by $B$ is $H$-stable, and $$\label{eq:reduced} B\text{ covers a nilpotent block of }H_0\trianglelefteq H \quad\Longrightarrow\quad H_0\leq Z(H)O_p(H).$$
- **Teichmüller subgroup** ([source](#pm-h-16)): Write $$\mathcal T=[k^\times]\subseteq\mathcal O^\times$$ for the Teichmüller subgroup.
- **Principal central units** ([source](#stmt-lem-center-units)): Then $Z_R$ is local with residue field $k$, and $$\label{eq:center-units} Z_R^\times=\mathcal T\times U_R, \qquad U_R=1+\operatorname{rad}Z_R.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| q-defect-order | Defect group order | ≤ | $M$ | The prime and defect bound are fixed while the group and block vary. |
| q-cartan-sum | Cartan sum | ≤ | $c(p,M)$ | The constant depends on the prime and defect bound. |
| q-basic-order-rank | Rank of the basic order | = | $\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}$ | The entries are those of the block's Cartan matrix. |
| q-positive-teichmuller-cohomology | Positive-degree cohomology with Teichmüller coefficients | = | $0$ | Finite groups of prime-power order and positive cohomological degrees. |
| q-extension-block-defect | Defect group order of a restriction block | ≤ | $\|S\|\,\|D\cap\bar N\|$ | The subgroup and the defect of the identity block are controlled by the fixed prime and defect bound. |
| q-restriction-frobenius-number | Integral Morita–Frobenius number of the restriction block | ≤ | $m$ | The comparison exponent is bounded in the prime and original defect bound. |
| q-basic-crossed-corner-rank | Rank of a compressed subgroup restriction | = | $\|S\|\operatorname{rank}_{\mathcal O}R$ | The identity order and abstract subgroup are fixed for the rank argument. |
| q-normal-kernel-index | Index of the isolated normal kernel | ≤ | $[Q:O_{p'}(Q)]\|\mathcal F\|$ | The normal kernel is the intersection of the normal subgroup of order prime to the residue characteristic with the outer-action kernel. |
| q-determinant-scalar-torsion | Power of the matrix comparison scalar | = | $1$ | The exponent is the matrix size, which is prime to the residue characteristic. |

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **step-cartan-and-integral-criterion** [claim](#stmt-prop-criteria) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-criteria): Transfer the finite field Morita list to a Cartan bound, then invoke the integral criterion combining defect, Cartan and Frobenius bounds. -> Numerical conditions sufficient for integral Morita finiteness of actual blocks.
- **step-controlled-crossed-realization** [claim](#stmt-prop-structure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-structure): Use the structural, partial-extension and dual-recovery inputs for an already reduced pair, retaining actual multiplication factors and full corners. -> Controlled identity orders and quotient indices for the relevant crossed orders.
- **step-integral-orbits** [claim](#stmt-lem-orbit) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-orbit): Use saturation and a unit minor to bound special-fibre orbits, then Hensel slices with isolated generic points to bound integral representatives. -> Finitely many integral orbits among the specified sections.
- **step-block-hochschild-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-lem-hh) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hh): Apply Shapiro's lemma and Morita invariance to derivations, then integral orbit finiteness to the automorphism scheme. -> Vanishing first Hochschild cohomology and finite outer automorphism group.
- **step-fixed-total-gradings** [claim](#stmt-thm-gradings) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-gradings): Describe labelled gradings by projections. Cancellation in the grading group turns every grading tangent into a derivation, which is inner by hypothesis. -> Finitely many labelled grading orbits under inner automorphisms.
- **step-recover-identity-markings** [claim](#stmt-prop-marked) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-marked): Bound projective-generator multiplicities using total rank; classify gradings on each resulting total order; absorb inner marking changes and retain finitely many outer ones. -> Finite based graded isomorphism list.
- **step-teichmuller-vanishing** [claim](#stmt-lem-teich-cohomology) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-teich-cohomology): Compare annihilation of positive cohomology by the group order with invertibility of the same power operation on the coefficient group. -> Vanishing positive-degree Teichmüller cohomology.
- **step-exact-integral-comparison** [claim](#stmt-prop-comparison) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-comparison): Track presentation errors through component products and central descent, prove they are Teichmüller-valued, and remove the scalar cocycle without changing the actual factors. -> An exact integral comparison with bounded coefficient-Frobenius exponent.
- **step-induce-crossed-equivalence** [claim](#stmt-lem-induced) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-induced): Induce the Morita module to the crossed order, define the left homogeneous operators, and identify its endomorphism order using the exact factor law. -> Morita equivalence of the crossed total orders.
- **step-actual-restriction-blocks** [claim](#stmt-prop-sylow-block) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sylow-block): Construct the finite extension group from the actual automorphisms and factors. Identify the stable covering block, bound its defect and Frobenius return, and apply the numerical criterion. -> Finite integral Morita list of actual pure subgroup restriction blocks.
- **step-based-subgroup-lists** [claim](#stmt-thm-based-sylow) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-based-sylow): Compress the identity block by a full basic idempotent, apply marked rigidity at fixed rank, and transport arbitrary subgroups to pure ones by homogeneous conjugation. -> Finite based lists for all relevant subgroup restrictions.
- **step-central-unit-splitting** [claim](#stmt-lem-center-units) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-center-units): Use henselian local decomposition and indecomposability, split the residue units by Teichmüller scalars, and apply Hensel lifting for powers prime to the residue characteristic. -> Canonical unit decomposition and unique divisibility of principal units by prime-to-characteristic powers.
- **step-finite-restriction-fibres** [claim](#stmt-lem-restriction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-restriction): Apply restriction and corestriction to obtain principal-unit injectivity, and use finite torsion coefficients to bound the Teichmüller contribution. -> Finite kernel for restriction on central-unit cohomology.
- **step-remove-normal-kernel** [claim](#stmt-thm-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-kernel): Isolate a twisted group algebra of the normal kernel, take matrix corners, normalize implementing determinants to preserve based Sylow factors, and count central factor systems by finite restriction fibres. -> Finite Morita list for block summands of the entire crossed-order family.
- **step-assemble-integral-finiteness** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-thm-kernel-2): Reduce an arbitrary block, compress its crossed realization to a listed basic identity order, verify the kernel-removal hypotheses, and take the finite union. -> Integral Donovan finiteness over the Witt ring.
- **step-reduce-basic-orders** [claim](#stmt-cor-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-reduction): Lift block and basic idempotents, apply the finite integral basic-order list, and reduce its corners. -> A finite integral list whose reductions represent all field basic algebras in the bounded-defect family.
- **step-fixed-ring-scalar-extension** [claim](#stmt-cor-complete-dvr) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-reduction-2): Choose a coefficient embedding, preserve primitive residue blocks and defect groups under field extension, identify compatible central lifts, and extend the inverse Morita bimodules. -> Finite Morita list over the fixed complete coefficient ring.

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **invariant-bounded-defect-morita-finiteness**: For a fixed prime and defect-order bound, finite-group blocks over the specified Witt ring have finitely many integral Morita equivalence classes.
- **invariant-fixed-total-grading-rigidity**: A fixed integral order with vanishing first Hochschild cohomology has finitely many labelled gradings by a fixed finite group, even up to inner automorphism.
- **invariant-based-marking-retention**: For crossed orders on a fixed basic block order with a fixed grading group, finite total Morita types imply finite based graded isomorphism types when the total orders are Morita equivalent to finite-group blocks.
- **invariant-teichmuller-cohomology-vanishing**: Positive-degree cohomology vanishes for a finite prime-power group acting trivially on the Teichmüller subgroup.
- **invariant-kernel-removal-conditions**: Integral kernel removal gives finite Morita types of block summands under bounded index over the normal prime-to-characteristic core, finite outer-action image, and finite based restrictions on prime-power subgroups.
- **invariant-fixed-coefficient-extension**: Bounded-defect Morita finiteness passes to a fixed complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero with algebraically closed residue field, including ramified rings.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **antiuse-varying-total-order**: Classify all crossed orders using only a fixed identity order and grading group. -> The total orders may vary through an infinite family; grading rigidity on a fixed total order does not establish finiteness for that family.
- **antiuse-discarded-multiplication-factors**: Recover a crossed order from its identity component and outer action alone. -> The multiplication factors carry additional data required by the crossed identities and by extension of a comparison bimodule.
- **antiuse-arbitrary-unit-cohomology**: Remove an arbitrary scalar-unit error on a prime-power subgroup by the Teichmüller cohomology argument. -> Principal-unit errors need not vanish on such a subgroup.
- **antiuse-integral-finite-point-count**: Infer integral finiteness by counting coefficient-Frobenius-fixed points as though their coordinate ring were finite. -> The fixed Witt-vector coefficient ring is infinite, so the finite-field counting argument does not establish integral finiteness.
- **antiuse-unbounded-defect**: Apply the main finiteness conclusion uniformly to families with growing defect. -> The theorem controls a family only after fixing a defect-order bound; it supplies no conclusion for the union over unbounded defect.
- **antiuse-varying-coefficient-rings**: Treat the complete-coefficient-ring corollary as a uniform classification across varying rings. -> Its finite Morita list is formed over a coefficient ring fixed in advance.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main result establishes qualitative Morita finiteness while quantifying over all finite groups and their bounded-defect blocks. It does not provide a finite enumeration procedure or an explicit complete list of representatives. The source-bound obstruction to a finite point-count certificate is stated under pm-h-4: “A coefficient-Frobenius-fixed point over $\mathcal O$ has coordinates in $W(\mathbb F_q)$, which is infinite.” The integral orbit, rigidity and cohomological arguments establish finiteness without reducing the main theorem to a finite executable test. Although the proof constructs particular finite group extensions, testing those constructions would not decide the global finiteness assertion.

## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Integral Morita finiteness supplies a mathematical classification result, but does not provide a procedure for computing representatives or deciding equivalence of supplied block orders. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The finite extension-group construction describes multiplication in an algebraic group extension; it supplies no simulation dynamics, game state model, or numerical evolution rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Scalar extension transfers the finiteness result to a fixed complete coefficient ring. It does not specify an effective representation of arbitrary coefficient elements or an executable coefficient-conversion procedure. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Integral orbit finiteness proves that the indicated points occupy finitely many orbits. It does not supply orbit representatives, a search bound, or a stopping certificate for enumeration. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Based graded isomorphism classes retain the identity marking and homogeneous labels, but the finiteness theorem supplies neither canonical encodings nor a method for computing a complete table of those classes. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Matrix factors are used to establish Morita equivalences, and their sizes need not be bounded. The argument provides no hardware mapping, bounded matrix workload, or accelerator resource contract. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The cohomological vanishing concerns a finite group acting trivially on the specified Teichmüller coefficient group. It supplies no cryptographic primitive or security reduction and cannot eliminate arbitrary unit-valued obstructions. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The grading theorem concerns a fixed integral order with vanishing first Hochschild cohomology and a fixed finite grading group. No learning objective, training procedure, or statistical generalization statement is constructed. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Finite fibres of Sylow restriction control algebraic factor-system classes. They do not define an objective function or provide a method for selecting an optimal representative. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Extending a comparison bimodule requires exact covariance and exact multiplication-factor identities. These algebraic compatibility requirements do not construct a distributed protocol or establish fault tolerance. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper directly supports integral representation theory through finite lists of basic orders. Its existential list of reductions does not provide an effective construction of all representatives, so a finite computational engineering contract is absent. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-fixed-prime-bounded-defect** (hypothesis): The prime and defect-order bound are fixed before the finite groups and blocks vary. The conclusion does not cover the union of families with unbounded defect. Blocked use: ['Assert a uniform finite Morita list for blocks with unrestricted defect.']
- **limit-fixed-complete-coefficient-ring** (hypothesis): The coefficient-ring extension requires a fixed complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero with algebraically closed residue field of the specified characteristic. Blocked use: ['Apply the corollary uniformly across varying coefficient rings.', 'Transfer the conclusion to incomplete rings or rings with residue fields outside the stated hypotheses.']
- **limit-integral-point-count** (formal_scope): Frobenius-fixed Witt-vector coordinates lie in an infinite ring, so a finite-field point count does not establish integral finiteness. Blocked use: ['Replace integral rigidity by counting Frobenius-fixed coefficient points.']
- **limit-actual-block-numerical-criterion** (hypothesis): The numerical integral finiteness criterion applies to finite-group blocks with bounded defect order, Cartan sum and integral Morita–Frobenius number. Identity-order finiteness alone does not supply these conditions for arbitrary crossed orders. Blocked use: ['Apply the numerical criterion to an arbitrary crossed order before establishing its block realization and the required bounds.']
- **limit-retain-crossed-multiplication** (formal_scope): A crossed order retains multiplication factors as well as automorphisms. Its identity component and outer action do not determine its multiplication. Blocked use: ['Discard factor elements when comparing or reconstructing crossed orders.']
- **limit-reduced-pair-input** (hypothesis): The controlled extension construction assumes a reduced pair. The preliminary integral reduction must preserve the defect group and Morita class before that construction is applied. Blocked use: ['Apply the controlled structural conclusions directly to an arbitrary unreduced pair.']
- **limit-integral-orbit-derivative** (hypothesis): Integral orbit finiteness requires a smooth affine acting group scheme of finite type and surjectivity of the integral orbit derivative at the points being counted. Blocked use: ['Infer finite integral orbit classes from finite type alone.', 'Replace the integral derivative condition by an unsupported special-fibre tangent condition.']
- **limit-fixed-total-grading-rigidity** (hypothesis): Grading rigidity fixes the total order and finite grading group and requires vanishing first Hochschild cohomology. Fixing only the identity order permits varying total orders and does not invoke this theorem. Blocked use: ['Deduce finiteness of all crossed orders over a fixed identity order from grading rigidity.']
- **limit-marked-total-morita-list** (hypothesis): Retention of the identity marking requires a fixed basic block order, a fixed grading group, block Morita realizations of the total orders and a finite total Morita list. Blocked use: ['Infer based graded finiteness merely from a finite identity-order list.', 'Replace based isomorphisms by unmarked equivalences when comparing restrictions.']
- **limit-teichmuller-trivial-action** (hypothesis): The cohomological vanishing uses a finite group of prime-power order acting trivially on the Teichmüller subgroup. Arbitrary principal-unit errors are outside this vanishing statement. Blocked use: ['Remove an unrestricted scalar-unit cocycle by the Teichmüller vanishing lemma.', 'Omit verification of the scalar coefficient group or its trivial action.']
- **limit-comparison-central-descent** (formal_scope): The auxiliary covering kernels and added central factors have no stated order bound. Comparison must descend to the original identity blocks before the bounded-defect criterion is applied. Blocked use: ['Assume the auxiliary central covers have bounded defect because the original block does.', 'Apply the numerical criterion before the central descent.']
- **limit-exact-crossed-comparison** (hypothesis): Extension of the comparison bimodule requires exact covariance and exact multiplication-factor identities over the integral coefficient ring. Blocked use: ['Extend a comparison known to satisfy the identities only after reduction.', 'Use a projective transport with an unresolved scalar discrepancy as an exact crossed action.']
- **limit-pure-restriction-block-construction** (hypothesis): The actual extension-block construction uses the prescribed group automorphisms and actual factor elements for a pure subgroup. Abstract outer-action data alone do not supply its group law. Blocked use: ['Declare an arbitrary graded restriction to be a finite-group block without the required extension data.']
- **limit-local-centre-and-principal-powers** (hypothesis): The stated central-unit decomposition assumes an indecomposable order. Unique divisibility of principal units is asserted for powers prime to the residue characteristic. Blocked use: ['Apply the local-centre conclusion to an arbitrary decomposable order.', 'Assume principal units are uniquely divisible by the residue characteristic.']
- **limit-sylow-restriction-finite-kernel** (formal_scope): Restriction to a Sylow subgroup has finite kernel on central-unit cohomology. The result does not assert that this kernel is trivial or that all central-unit cohomology vanishes. Blocked use: ['Replace finite restriction fibres by unique lifts of restricted factor-system classes.', 'Apply the restriction lemma to an arbitrary subgroup without the Sylow condition.']
- **limit-kernel-removal-hypotheses** (hypothesis): Kernel removal fixes the indecomposable basic identity order and finite outer-action subgroup. It also requires bounded index over the normal subgroup of order prime to the residue characteristic and finite based restrictions for every possible abstract subgroup of prime-power order. Blocked use: ['Remove the normal kernel using only a bounded quotient index.', 'Substitute finite unmarked Morita restrictions for finite based graded restrictions.']
- **limit-matrix-correction-prime-to-characteristic** (hypothesis): Preservation of based Sylow restrictions after matrix correction uses matrix size prime to the residue characteristic, determinant normalization and Teichmüller-valued scalar error. Blocked use: ['Assume arbitrary matrix corrections preserve the original multiplication factors.', 'Use determinant normalization without establishing the required prime-to-characteristic matrix size.']
- **limit-no-effective-global-enumeration** (nonconstructive): The global finiteness argument supplies no explicit complete representative list, enumeration stopping certificate or computational complexity bound. Those computational contracts are ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Infer a terminating classification procedure from qualitative Morita finiteness.', 'Treat finite tests of extension-group multiplication as certification of the global theorem.']
- **limit-dependency-direction** (formal_scope): The field companion supplies the Cartan input before the integral theorem is proved. The reduction corollary is a consequence of the integral theorem and cannot replace that earlier input without introducing circular reasoning. Blocked use: ['Use the integral reduction corollary as the premise establishing the Cartan input for the same integral proof.']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove integral Donovan finiteness: for every prime <i>p</i> and positive integer <i>M</i>, the blocks of all finite groups with defect groups of order at most <i>M</i> have only finitely many Morita equivalence classes over $`W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)`$. The defect groups need not be abelian, and the result includes <i>p</i> = 2. The same bounded-defect finiteness holds over each fixed complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero with algebraically closed residue field of characteristic <i>p</i>, including ramified rings.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>

**Article identifier:** `Integral-Donovan-Finiteness-over-Witt-Vectors-September-25-2026`

<a id="sec:introduction"></a>
### Introduction

Fix a prime $p$, put $k=\overline{\mathbb F}_p$, and let $\mathcal O=W(k)$ be its ring of Witt vectors. A block of a finite group $G$ over $\mathcal O$ is an algebra $\mathcal OG b$, where $b$ is a primitive central idempotent. Its reduction $kG\bar b$ has a defect group, well defined up to conjugacy, and we use the same defect group for the integral block. All Morita equivalences in this paper are equivalences of categories of finitely generated modules which are linear over the specified coefficient ring.

Fixing the defect group is expected to leave only finitely many Morita types of blocks. This is Donovan’s finiteness problem. Over $\mathcal O$ it concerns integral module categories, including their lattice structure, and hence is stronger than the corresponding assertion over the residue field. Our main theorem is the integral assertion for the Witt ring $W(k)$.

<a id="stmt-thm-main"></a>
<div id="thm:main" class="theorem">

**Theorem 1**. *Let $p$ be a prime, let $\mathcal O=W(\overline{\mathbb F}_p)$, and let $M\geq1$ be an integer. As $G$ ranges over all finite groups and $b$ ranges over all blocks of $\mathcal OG$ whose defect groups have order at most $M$, the algebras $\mathcal OG b$ belong to only finitely many $\mathcal O$-linear Morita equivalence classes.*

</div>

The theorem includes every prime, including $2$, and every block, including nonprincipal blocks. The defect groups may be nonabelian; the ambient finite groups have no order bound. There are finitely many groups of order at most $M$, so fixing one defect group or bounding its order gives equivalent finiteness assertions. In terms of basic orders, Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a> says that a finite list of $\mathcal O$-algebras represents all the indicated Morita classes.

The finiteness conclusion also passes to a fixed complete coefficient ring with algebraically closed residue field.

<a id="stmt-cor-complete-dvr"></a>
<div id="cor:complete-dvr" class="corollary">

**Corollary 2** (Fixed complete coefficient rings). *Let $p$ be a prime, let $\mathcal R$ be a fixed complete discrete valuation ring of characteristic zero whose residue field $\ell$ is algebraically closed of characteristic $p$, and let $M\geq1$ be an integer. As $G$ ranges over all finite groups and $b$ ranges over primitive central idempotents of $\mathcal R G$ for which the residue block $\ell G\bar b$ has a defect group of order at most $M$, the algebras $\mathcal R G b$ belong to only finitely many $\mathcal R$-linear Morita equivalence classes.*

</div>

Here $\mathcal R$ is fixed before $G$ and $b$ vary. It may be ramified, and no splitting hypothesis on its fraction field is required. The proof at the end of Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">7</a> uses compatible block idempotent lifts and scalar extension of the integral Morita equivalences from Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

<a id="pm-h-3"></a>
#### Finiteness criteria and extension methods

The local representation-theoretic questions surrounding Donovan’s conjecture were articulated in Alperin’s account of local representation theory (Alperin 1980) <a id="pm-cite-1"></a>[[Alperin1980](#ref-Alperin1980)]. Two different sorts of control enter finiteness: bounds on the size of a basic algebra, and bounds on its field of definition. Hiss made the field-of-definition requirement explicit: bounded defect and Cartan invariants yield Morita finiteness when the blocks have split forms over a common finite field (Hiss 2000, Proposition 5.1) <a id="pm-cite-2"></a>[[HissBrauer2000](#ref-HissBrauer2000)]. Kessar separated these issues using Morita–Frobenius numbers (Kessar 2004) <a id="pm-cite-3"></a>[[KessarRemark2004](#ref-KessarRemark2004)]. For an integral order $B$, its Morita–Frobenius number $\operatorname{mf}_{\mathcal O}(B)$ is the least positive coefficient-Frobenius power giving a Morita-equivalent order. The integral size and rationality bounds are combined in the finiteness theorem of Eaton–Eisele–Livesey (Eaton, Eisele, and Livesey 2020, Theorem 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-4"></a>[[EEL2020](#ref-EEL2020)]: bounded defect, bounded Cartan sum, and bounded integral Morita–Frobenius number imply integral Morita finiteness. The theorem itself allows arbitrary defect groups; the Cartan-only quasisimple reduction in that paper has the additional hypothesis of abelian defect. Their results establish integral Donovan finiteness for all abelian $2$-groups (Eaton, Eisele, and Livesey 2020, Corollary 4.6) <a id="pm-cite-5"></a>[[EEL2020](#ref-EEL2020)]. For nonabelian defect groups, Eaton–Eisele–Kessar–Linckelmann–Schaeffer Fry prove integral Donovan finiteness for quaternion defect groups (Eaton et al. 2026, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-6"></a>[[EEKLS2026](#ref-EEKLS2026)].

Crossed products encode the passage from normal subgroups to their extensions. Külshammer developed this approach in Clifford theory and in his reduction of Donovan’s conjecture (Külshammer 1990, 1995) <a id="pm-cite-7"></a>[[KulshammerClifford1990](#ref-KulshammerClifford1990), [Kulshammer1995](#ref-Kulshammer1995)]. Eisele established integral versions and studied the relevant Picard groups (Eisele 2021, 2022) <a id="pm-cite-8"></a>[[EiseleReduction2021](#ref-EiseleReduction2021), [EiseleGeometry2022](#ref-EiseleGeometry2022)]. His geometry of rigid lattices supplies the methodological setting for integral Picard finiteness (Eisele 2022, Theorems A and B) <a id="pm-cite-9"></a>[[EiseleGeometry2022](#ref-EiseleGeometry2022)]. We use the preliminary Fong and nilpotent-block reduction in An–Eaton (An and Eaton 2025, Proposition 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-10"></a>[[AnEaton2025](#ref-AnEaton2025)]; that proposition applies to arbitrary defect groups over $\mathcal O$, independently of the extraspecial hypotheses used in their subsequent results.

For quasisimple groups, Farrell–Kessar give the uniform bound $\operatorname{mf}_{\mathcal O}(B)\leq4$ (Farrell and Kessar 2019, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-11"></a>[[FarrellKessar2019](#ref-FarrellKessar2019)]. Their theorem does not itself compare the multiplication factors in an arbitrary group extension. This distinction matters: controlling the Morita class of an identity component or its outer automorphisms does not determine a crossed product. Eisele–Livesey’s constructions of arbitrarily large Morita–Frobenius numbers, in families with growing defect, give further reason to retain the rationality data explicitly (Eisele and Livesey 2022) <a id="pm-cite-12"></a>[[EiseleLivesey2022](#ref-EiseleLivesey2022)].

The companion article *Donovan’s Conjecture over Algebraically Closed Fields* (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-13"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)] establishes bounded-defect finiteness over $k$ for all finite groups. It supplies two inputs here: a uniform Cartan bound, through its field theorem, and the integral extension and comparison constructions of its Sections 8 and 9. The latter retain the actual multiplication factors in the normal-subgroup extensions. Their advertised equivariance is exact after reduction. We will refine the specified integral operators to obtain exact equivariance over $\mathcal O$ itself.

<a id="pm-h-4"></a>
#### What must be retained over the Witt ring

The distinction between the two coefficient rings is substantial. A coefficient-Frobenius-fixed point over $\mathcal O$ has coordinates in $W(\mathbb F_q)$, which is infinite. Thus the finite-field point count used in a descent argument over $k$ gives no integral finiteness assertion. Also, a scalar obstruction over $\mathcal O$ may contain principal units, whose cohomology on a $p$-group need not vanish. We address these two issues separately: the first requires rigidity of integral orders, and the second requires control of the particular scalar errors produced by the comparison.

The normal-subgroup reductions make every relevant block Morita equivalent to a block summand of a crossed order with identity component an integral block $B_0$ and grading group $Q$. The integral basic orders of $B_0$ belong to a finite list, while $[Q:O_{p'}(Q)]$ is bounded. A crossed order retains both the automorphisms of $B_0$ and the units that occur when homogeneous generators are multiplied. Its restriction to a subgroup is the sum of the homogeneous components labelled by the elements of that subgroup.

Three successive assertions carry the integral proof. The first is *exact comparison*. The geometric overlap operators have discrepancies given by prime-to-$p$ character values, so these discrepancies lie in the Teichmüller subgroup $\mathcal T=[k^\times]\subseteq\mathcal O^\times$. We check this membership through component products, central quotients and the prescribed inner factors. Only then do we use the vanishing of positive cohomology of a finite $p$-group with coefficients in $\mathcal T$. The resulting integral comparison extends to every Sylow restriction, giving a Morita equivalence with a bounded coefficient-Frobenius twist.

The second assertion is *based finiteness*. Each pure Sylow restriction is an actual finite-group block of bounded defect; we construct its group from the given automorphisms and factor elements. The field companion bounds its Cartan sum, so the integral criterion of Eaton–Eisele–Livesey gives finitely many integral Morita classes for these total restriction orders. To preserve their labelled homogeneous components and the chosen identity component, we prove an integral orbit lemma. Applied to the scheme of gradings of a fixed order with vanishing first Hochschild cohomology, it gives finitely many group-labelled gradings, even when $p$ divides the grading-group order. The word “fixed” refers to the *total* order; this is different from counting all crossed products of a fixed identity order. We retain the chosen identification of the identity component; isomorphisms preserving that identification are called *based*.

The third assertion removes the possibly unbounded normal $p'$-kernel of $Q$ while retaining those based restrictions. Its twisted group algebra splits into matrix algebras over $\mathcal O$ of degree prime to $p$. Determinant-one choices of the matrices implementing a Sylow action force the new scalar discrepancy to be Teichmüller-valued. Thus the Sylow restrictions remain in their controlled based list. Restriction and corestriction control the principal-unit contribution on the remaining bounded quotient, and its Teichmüller cohomology is finite. A finite-fibre argument for the central factor systems then gives the integral Morita list.

<a id="pm-h-5"></a>
#### Organization and dependencies

Section <a href="#sec:inputs" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:inputs">2</a> fixes the crossed-product conventions and states the imported block constructions. The integral rigidity results in Section <a href="#sec:rigidity" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:rigidity">3</a> are independent of the comparison construction. Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">4</a> proves the exact integral comparison. These two arguments meet in Section <a href="#sec:sylow" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:sylow">5</a>: the comparison gives finite total Morita types of actual extension blocks, and rigidity recovers their labelled components and markings. Section <a href="#sec:kernel" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:kernel">6</a> removes the large normal $p'$-kernel, and Section <a href="#sec:assembly" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:assembly">7</a> proves Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>.

The field theorem is used before the integral theorem, in the Cartan input and the specified companion constructions. The reduction corollary records that the resulting finite integral list also represents all field basic algebras; it is not a premise of the argument. The fixed-coefficient-ring corollary is then proved by scalar extension.

<a id="sec:inputs"></a>
### Integral orders and the imported block constructions

The proof requires numerical bounds for actual blocks and a precise description of the crossed orders produced by reduction. We state these inputs separately. The distinction will matter when the numerical criterion is applied: first we identify a restriction as a group block, and then we use its Cartan and Frobenius bounds.

<a id="pm-h-7"></a>
#### Orders, Frobenius and the numerical criterion

An $\mathcal O$-order means a unital $\mathcal O$-algebra free of finite rank as an $\mathcal O$-module. For a general order, a block summand means its direct factor at a primitive central idempotent. Put $K_0=\operatorname{Frac}(\mathcal O)$ and let $\sigma$ be Witt Frobenius. For an order $A$, the coefficient twist $\sigma^m A$ is the order obtained by applying $\sigma^m$ to its structure constants; equivalently it is scalar transport along $\sigma^m$. For a group block we identify it with $\mathcal OG\sigma^m(b)$. We write $\operatorname{mf}_{\mathcal O}(A)$ for the least positive $m$ for which $A$ and $\sigma^m A$ are $\mathcal O$-linearly Morita equivalent, whenever such an $m$ exists. Only upper bounds for this number will be used.

Every finite $\mathcal O$-algebra is semiperfect. A basic idempotent of a block order is a sum of one primitive idempotent for every isomorphism type of indecomposable projective. It is full, and its corner is the basic order. Basic orders are unique up to isomorphism within a Morita class. If the Cartan matrix of a block is $(c_{ij})$, its basic order has $\mathcal O$-rank $\sum_{i,j}c_{ij}$: reduction is a split basic $k$-algebra, whose dimension is that sum.

<a id="stmt-prop-criteria"></a>
<div id="prop:criteria" class="proposition">

**Proposition 3** (Cartan and integral finiteness inputs). *The following assertions will be used.*

1.  *For every $p$ and $M$, the Cartan sums of all finite-group blocks over $\mathcal O$ with defect order at most $M$ are bounded by a constant $c(p,M)$.*

2.  *For fixed positive bounds on the defect order, Cartan sum, and integral Morita–Frobenius number, finite-group blocks over $\mathcal O$ have only finitely many $\mathcal O$-linear Morita equivalence classes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-criteria"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-criteria</summary>

*Proof.* By (OpenAI 2026a, Theorem 1.1) <a id="pm-cite-14"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)], the reductions of the blocks in (i) have finitely many $k$-Morita classes. Their Cartan matrices, up to simultaneous permutation, consequently form a finite list. Integral blocks and their reductions have the same Cartan matrix, which proves (i).

Assertion (ii) is (Eaton, Eisele, and Livesey 2020, Theorem 3.10) <a id="pm-cite-15"></a>[[EEL2020](#ref-EEL2020)], applied to the finitely many possible defect exponents. The coefficient conventions of that theorem include the absolutely unramified complete discrete valuation ring $W(k)$ and its coefficient Frobenius, which fixes the uniformizer $p$. Its defect-zero case can also be separated: a defect-zero block over $W(k)$ is a matrix algebra over $W(k)$ and has basic order $\mathcal O$. Indeed, its reduction is a full matrix algebra over $k$; lift its matrix units over the complete ring $\mathcal O$, whose resulting primitive corner has rank one. ◻

</details>

<a id="pm-h-8"></a>
#### Multiplication factors and identity markings

<a id="stmt-auto-definition-1"></a>
<div class="definition">

<a id="eq:action-law"></a>
<a id="eq:factor-law"></a>
**Definition 4** (Crossed orders and based isomorphisms). Let $R$ be an $\mathcal O$-order and $Q$ a finite group. A normalized crossed system consists of $\alpha_q\in\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathcal O}(R)$ and $a_{q,t}\in R^\times$ such that $$\begin{aligned}
 \alpha_q\alpha_t&=\operatorname{ad}(a_{q,t})\alpha_{qt},
       \label{eq:action-law}\\
 a_{q,t}a_{qt,v}&=\alpha_q(a_{t,v})a_{q,tv},
       \label{eq:factor-law}
\end{aligned}$$ with $\alpha_1=\mathrm{id}$ and $a_{1,q}=a_{q,1}=1$. Its crossed order is $A=\bigoplus_{q\in Q}Ru_q$, with $$u_qr=\alpha_q(r)u_q,\qquad
 u_qu_t=a_{q,t}u_{qt},\qquad u_1=1.$$ A $Q$-labelled graded isomorphism preserves each homogeneous component with its label. When the identity component is identified with a fixed $R$, such an isomorphism is *based* if it is the identity on $R$.

</div>

Changing each $u_q$ to $t_qu_q$, with $t_q\in R^\times$ and $t_1=1$, gives exactly the based changes of crossed systems. The induced homomorphism $Q\to\operatorname{Out}_{\mathcal O}(R)$ is the outer action. It remembers the actions only modulo inner automorphisms. Both the units implementing their products in Equation <a href="#eq:action-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:action-law">[eq:action-law]</a> and the compatibility in Equation <a href="#eq:factor-law" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:factor-law">[eq:factor-law]</a> are needed to recover the order. For $S\leq Q$ the *restriction to $S$* is the crossed suborder $A_S=\bigoplus_{s\in S}Ru_s$, with the same identity marking and the restricted factors.

<a id="pm-h-9"></a>
#### Reduction and dual recovery

We call a pair $(H,B)$ reduced if $B$ is a block of $\mathcal OH$ such that every block of a normal subgroup covered by $B$ is $H$-stable, and <a id="eq:reduced"></a>$$\label{eq:reduced}
 B\text{ covers a nilpotent block of }H_0\trianglelefteq H
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad H_0\leq Z(H)O_p(H).$$ An–Eaton’s preliminary reduction (An and Eaton 2025, Proposition 6.1) <a id="pm-cite-16"></a>[[AnEaton2025](#ref-AnEaton2025)] replaces an arbitrary block by a reduced pair through an $\mathcal O$-linear basic Morita equivalence, preserving the defect group. Their coefficient conventions include $\mathcal O=W(k)$. We apply this integral reduction directly.

The next proposition collects the group structure, actual factor elements and integral full corners supplied by the field companion. Constants in it depend only on $p,M$.

<a id="stmt-prop-structure"></a>
<div id="prop:structure" class="proposition">

**Proposition 5** (Controlled integral extensions). *Let $(H,B)$ be a reduced pair with defect order at most $M$. Put $N=F^*(H)$, $X=H/N$, and let $b$ be the block of $\mathcal ON$ covered by $B$. Then the following constructions and bounds hold.*

1.  *The group $N$ is a central product $$N=PZK_1\cdots K_j,\qquad P=O_p(H),\quad Z=O_{p'}(H)\leq Z(H),$$ where the $K_i$ are quasisimple components, $|P|$ and $j$ are bounded, and $[X:O_{p'}(X)]$ is bounded. The latter index is bounded also for every subgroup of every extension of $X$ by a $p'$-group.*

<a id="eq:actual-actions"></a>
<a id="eq:actual-factors"></a>
2.  *There is an extension $N\trianglelefteq\bar N$ with abelian $p'$-quotient $A_0=\bar N/N$. For representatives $h_x$ of $x\in X$, normalized by $h_1=1$, write $h_xh_y=n_{x,y}h_{xy}$ with $n_{x,y}\in N$. Conjugation extends to automorphisms $\alpha_x$ of $\bar N$ satisfying the actual identities $$\begin{aligned}
     \alpha_x\alpha_y&=\operatorname{ad}(n_{x,y})\alpha_{xy},
           \label{eq:actual-actions}\\
     n_{x,y}n_{xy,z}&=\alpha_x(n_{y,z})n_{x,yz}.
           \label{eq:actual-factors}
    \end{aligned}$$ Every block $\bar b$ of $\mathcal O\bar N$ covering $b$ has bounded defect.*

3.  *Put $\Xi=\operatorname{Hom}(A_0,k^\times)$, using Teichmüller lifts of the characters over $\mathcal O$, and $Y=\Xi\rtimes X$. There is a $Y$-crossed order with identity component $\mathcal O\bar N$ in which $\mathcal OH$ is a full idempotent corner. After taking an appropriate central summand and a further full corner, this is a crossed order with identity component $$B_0=\mathcal O\bar N\bar b$$ and grading group $Q=\operatorname{Stab}_Y(\bar b)$. In the pure $X$-degrees the actions and factors are those in (ii). The index $[Q:O_{p'}(Q)]$ is bounded.*

4.  *The basic orders of all these identity blocks $B_0$ belong to a finite list of integral orders $R_1,\ldots,R_t$. Their integral Picard groups, and in particular their outer automorphism groups, are finite.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-structure"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-structure</summary>

*Source of the assertions.* For a reduced pair, the structural conclusions follow from (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 8.1) <a id="pm-cite-17"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)]. Its opening Morita reduction is stated over $k$; we use only its conclusions about the already reduced pair, obtained here by the integral An–Eaton reduction. The compatible partial extension and its actual factors are (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 8.2) <a id="pm-cite-18"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)]. The integral dual recovery construction and full corners are (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 8.3) <a id="pm-cite-19"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)].

For clarity, the dual-recovery idempotent is $|\Xi|^{-1}\sum_{\chi\in\Xi}u_\chi$. The characters are trivial on every $n_{x,y}\in N$, so the pure $X$-units preserve this idempotent and retain exactly these factors. This explains why the construction is integral and why it retains the multiplication data needed later. Finally (iv) is the integral assertion of (OpenAI 2026a, Lemma 8.5) <a id="pm-cite-20"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)], using the trivial-subgroup case of its Proposition 9.1. The finite Picard assertion is also (Eisele 2022, Theorem B and Corollary 1.2) <a id="pm-cite-21"></a>[[EiseleGeometry2022](#ref-EiseleGeometry2022)]. ◻

</details>

Every $p$-subgroup $S$ of $Y$ can be conjugated by an element of $\Xi$ into the pure subgroup $X$. Indeed, its image in $X$ is a $p$-group, and Schur–Zassenhaus conjugates the two complements to $\Xi$ in the inverse image of that group. In the crossed order this conjugation is implemented by an integral homogeneous unit; it also transports $\bar b$. We may therefore work with pure $p$-subgroups, provided we keep the transported identity block in the same family. In particular, the orders of the relevant $p$-subgroups of $Q$ are bounded: their intersection with $O_{p'}(Q)$ is trivial, so they inject into the quotient whose order was bounded in (iii).

There is no bound here on the orders of the central kernels in the universal-cover presentations, or on the extra central $p$-factors used in the partial regular extensions. The comparison in Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">4</a> descends through those kernels before any bounded-defect integral criterion is applied.

<a id="pm-h-10"></a>
#### The companion’s comparison data

The comparison convention in (OpenAI 2026a, Definition 8.4 and Proposition 9.1) <a id="pm-cite-22"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)] uses an integral Morita bimodule but asserts exact covariance and factor identities on its reduction. We use the particular integral operators constructed there. Their ingredients have four distinct roles. Lemmas 9.2–9.4 realize the genuine field, graph and inner relations, construct an invariant torus and character, and choose a common parabolic–Levi pair with the specified inner overlap. Lemma 9.5 gives an integral Levi Morita bimodule with strict geometric actions and matching central actions. Lemma 9.6 gives a uniformly bounded coefficient-Frobenius return and its prime-to-$p$ character twist. Finally, Lemma 9.7 computes the integral scalar overlap of the chosen operators.

Section <a href="#sec:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="sec:comparison">4</a> recalls these constructions with their exact hypotheses and shows that their scalar errors stay in $\mathcal T$. The subsequent normalization over $\mathcal O$ is proved here. Thus the imported comparison supplies operators and overlap identities, while Proposition <a href="#prop:comparison" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:comparison">12</a> supplies the exact integral factor law required by the later extension argument.

<a id="sec:rigidity"></a>
### Integral orbits and gradings of a fixed order

An ungraded Morita list does not specify the components of a crossed order. This section gives the additional rigidity needed to recover both their group labels and the identity marking. There are three steps: an orbit lemma over $\mathcal O$, a tangent calculation for gradings of a fixed total order, and a rank argument which passes from Morita classes to such fixed orders. The orbit method is related to the geometry of rigid lattices and Picard groups in (Eisele 2022) <a id="pm-cite-23"></a>[[EiseleGeometry2022](#ref-EiseleGeometry2022)]; the grading argument below works for every finite grading group.

<a id="pm-h-12"></a>
#### Integral orbit finiteness

<a id="stmt-lem-orbit"></a>
<div id="lem:orbit" class="lemma">

**Lemma 6** (Integral orbit finiteness). *Let $J$ be a smooth affine group scheme of finite type over $\mathcal O$, acting on an affine scheme $V$ of finite type over $\mathcal O$. For $x\in V(\mathcal O)$ let $T_xV$ denote the integral tangent module along the section $x$. The points for which the orbit derivative $$\operatorname{Lie}(J)\longrightarrow T_xV$$ is surjective belong to only finitely many $J(\mathcal O)$-orbits.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-orbit"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-orbit</summary>

*Proof.* We bound the special-fibre orbits and then cut each one by a fixed integral slice. The latter has only finitely many possible generic points arising from the sections under consideration.

*The integral tangent and horizontal components.* Embed $V$ as a closed subscheme of $\mathbb A^r_{\mathcal O}$, with finitely many defining equations. The module $T_xV$ is the kernel of their Jacobian map on $\mathcal O^r$. It is therefore saturated in $\mathcal O^r$. Write $u=\operatorname{rank}_{\mathcal O}T_xV$. Surjectivity of the orbit derivative and saturation imply that its coordinate matrix has a unit minor of size $u$. This follows, for example, from Smith normal form over the discrete valuation ring <a id="pm-cite-24"></a>[[StacksProject](#ref-StacksProject), Lemma 33.19.2, Tag 0B2J]$\mathcal O$.

<a id="eq:fibre-dimension"></a>
After extension to $K_0$, this derivative has rank $u$, which is also $\dim_{K_0}T_{x_{K_0}}V_{K_0}$. Every irreducible component of $V_{K_0}$ through $x_{K_0}$ consequently has dimension at most $u$. Let $V_u$ be the reduced closure in $V$ of the union of the generic-fibre irreducible components of dimension at most $u$. There are finitely many such components. Each of their horizontal closures has special fibre of dimension at most its generic-fibre dimension. This is the dimension theorem for an irreducible finite-type scheme over a valuation ring (The Stacks Project Authors 2026b, Lemma 33.19.2, Tag 0B2J). Thus $$\label{eq:fibre-dimension}
 \dim (V_u)_k\leq u.$$ The section $x$ factors through $V_u$, because its generic point does and $\mathcal O\hookrightarrow K_0$ is injective.

*The special-fibre orbits.* Let $x_0$ be the reduction of $x$. The unit minor remains nonzero modulo $p$, so the special-fibre orbit of $x_0$ has dimension at least $u$. This orbit lies in $(V_u)_k$. One way to verify that last assertion is to lift every element of $J(k)$ to $J(\mathcal O)$, using smoothness and completeness, and then apply it to the section $x$. The transformed generic point still lies on components of dimension at most $u$. Equation <a href="#eq:fibre-dimension" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:fibre-dimension">[eq:fibre-dimension]</a> now shows that the orbit has dimension exactly $u$.

A locally closed orbit of dimension $u$ in a scheme of dimension at most $u$ contains an open subset of an irreducible component of dimension $u$. Two distinct orbits cannot both contain dense open subsets of the same component. There are therefore finitely many possible special-fibre orbits for this $u$.

*A fixed Hensel slice.* Fix one such orbit and a representative $x_0$. By lifting elements of $J(k)$, move all integral points under consideration so that their reduction equals $x_0$. Choose $u$ coordinate functions whose orbit derivative has a nonzero $u$-minor at $x_0$, and fix integral lifts $a_1,\ldots,a_u$ of their values there. For each such integral point $x$, the map $$J\longrightarrow\mathbb A^u_{\mathcal O},\qquad
 g\longmapsto\text{the selected coordinates of }g x$$ is smooth near the identity: $J$ is smooth and its relative differential there is surjective. Hensel lifting supplies $g\equiv1\pmod p$ for which the selected coordinates of $gx$ are exactly the $a_i$. Thus every integral orbit has a representative in the fixed affine slice $$W=V\cap\{\text{selected coordinates }=a_1,\ldots,a_u\}.$$

For any resulting point $y$, the selected-coordinate differential is an isomorphism on $T_{y_{K_0}}V_{K_0}$: that space has dimension $u$, and the same minor is a unit since $y$ reduces to $x_0$. It follows that $T_{y_{K_0}}W_{K_0}=0$. Such a point is isolated in the finite-type $K_0$-scheme $W_{K_0}$. A noetherian scheme has only finitely many isolated points. Each generic point gives at most one integral section, again by the injection $\mathcal O\hookrightarrow K_0$. Hence there are finitely many representatives in this slice. Taking the finite union over the special-fibre orbits and the possible ranks $0\leq u\leq r$ proves the lemma. ◻

</details>

The integral tangent module in this proof need not reduce to the whole tangent space at $x_0$. Saturation and the unit minor are what the proof uses. In particular, no smoothness assumption on $V$, nor on an automorphism scheme occurring as $V$, is needed. The rank-zero case is included by using no slice coordinates. The argument uses smoothness of $J$ for lifting and for its differential; it does not require $J$ to be connected.

<a id="pm-h-13"></a>
#### Block rigidity and labelled gradings

<a id="stmt-lem-hh"></a>
<div id="lem:HH" class="lemma">

**Lemma 7** (Hochschild rigidity and outer automorphisms). *Let $A$ be an $\mathcal O$-order Morita equivalent to a finite direct sum of finite-group block orders. Then $\operatorname{HH}^1_{\mathcal O}(A)=0$, and $\operatorname{Out}_{\mathcal O}(A)$ is finite.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-lem-hh"><summary>Proof of stmt-lem-hh</summary>

*Proof.* For a finite group $G$, the conjugation permutation lattice and Shapiro’s lemma give $$\operatorname{HH}^1_{\mathcal O}(\mathcal OG)
 \cong H^1(G,\mathcal OG_{\mathrm{conj}})
 \cong\bigoplus_{[g]}H^1(C_G(g),\mathcal O)=0.$$ The action on the last coefficient module is trivial, and $\operatorname{Hom}(C_G(g),\mathcal O_{\mathrm{add}})=0$ because $C_G(g)$ is finite and $\mathcal O$ is torsion-free. Hochschild cohomology splits over finite direct products and is Morita invariant, proving the first assertion. Thus every $\mathcal O$-linear derivation of $A$ is inner.

The unit group scheme $A^\times$ is an open subscheme of the affine space underlying $A$, defined by invertibility of the regular-representation determinant. It is smooth. The automorphisms of $A$ form an affine scheme of finite type: impose the multiplicative and unital equations on an invertible linear map. Let $A^\times$ act by postcomposition with inner automorphisms. At an automorphism, compose with its inverse to identify the tangent module with the derivations of $A$. The orbit derivative then consists of the inner derivations, so it is surjective. Lemma <a href="#lem:orbit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:orbit">6</a> says exactly that there are finitely many cosets modulo inner automorphisms. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-thm-gradings"></a>
<div id="thm:gradings" class="theorem">

**Theorem 8** (Gradings of a fixed integral order). *Let $A$ be an $\mathcal O$-order with $\operatorname{HH}^1_{\mathcal O}(A)=0$, and let $H$ be any fixed finite group. There are only finitely many $H$-labelled gradings of $A$ up to $\mathcal O$-algebra automorphism. In fact there are only finitely many orbits under inner automorphisms.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-thm-gradings"><summary>Proof of stmt-thm-gradings</summary>

*Proof.* We apply Lemma <a href="#lem:orbit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:orbit">6</a> to the space of decompositions compatible with multiplication. The key point is that the tangent calculation uses cancellation in $H$, without averaging over $H$.

A grading $A=\bigoplus_{h\in H}A_h$ is specified by its orthogonal projections $e_h\in\operatorname{End}_{\mathcal O}(A)$. These satisfy $$\begin{aligned}
 e_he_j&=\delta_{h,j}e_h,& \sum_{h\in H}e_h&=1,\\
 e_h(1_A)&=\delta_{h,1}1_A,&
 e_l(e_g(x)e_h(y))&=0\quad(l\ne gh).
\end{aligned}$$ The last equations need only be imposed on a fixed finite basis of $A$. They define an affine finite-type grading scheme, on which $A^\times$ acts by conjugation.

We compute its integral tangent module at a grading. A first-order deformation of the direct-sum decomposition is uniquely represented by an off-diagonal $\mathcal O$-linear map $\delta:A\to A$, where $$e_h\delta e_h=0\quad(h\in H).$$ The deformed summands are $(1+\varepsilon\delta)A_h$ over $\mathcal O[\varepsilon]/(\varepsilon^2)$. This description follows by differentiating the equations for orthogonal idempotent projections, or by writing the summands as graphs over the original summands. It uses no division by $|H|$.

<a id="eq:grading-derivative"></a>
For $x\in A_g$ and $y\in A_h$, the linearized multiplicative equations say that $$\label{eq:grading-derivative}
 \delta(xy)-\delta(x)y-x\delta(y)$$ has zero component in every degree other than $gh$. It also has zero $gh$-component. The first term is off degree $gh$ by definition. A summand of $\delta(x)y$ can have degree $gh$ only if its first factor has degree $g$, by cancellation in $H$; that component of $\delta(x)$ is zero. The same reasoning applies to $x\delta(y)$. Hence Equation <a href="#eq:grading-derivative" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="eq:grading-derivative">[eq:grading-derivative]</a> vanishes, and $\delta$ is a derivation.

The hypothesis gives $\delta=[a,-]$ for some $a\in A$. Conjugation by $1+\varepsilon a$ produces the specified tangent to the grading. Thus the orbit derivative from the smooth group $A^\times$ is surjective onto every integral tangent module. Lemma <a href="#lem:orbit" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:orbit">6</a> proves the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="stmt-rem-fixed-total"></a>
<div id="rem:fixed-total" class="remark">

**Remark 9**. The total order is fixed in Theorem <a href="#thm:gradings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:gradings">8</a>. Finiteness of all crossed orders on a fixed identity order is a different assertion. For example, for odd $p$ the based $C_p$-crossed orders $$\mathcal O[t]/(t^p-a),\qquad a\in\mathcal O^\times,$$ have classes parametrized by $\mathcal O^\times/(\mathcal O^\times)^p$. The principal-unit quotient is infinite: the $p$-adic logarithm identifies $(1+p\mathcal O)/(1+p\mathcal O)^p$ with $p\mathcal O/p^2\mathcal O\cong k$. Their total orders vary. The crossed-product finiteness theorem in the published (Eisele 2021, Corollary 4.9) <a id="pm-cite-25"></a>[[EiseleReduction2021](#ref-EiseleReduction2021)] assumes a $p'$-grading group. Theorem <a href="#thm:gradings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:gradings">8</a> uses a different hypothesis and has just been proved also for groups divisible by $p$.

</div>

<a id="pm-h-14"></a>
#### From Morita classes to based graded types

For a crossed order on a fixed identity order, fixing the grading group also fixes the total rank. This converts a finite Morita list into a finite isomorphism list, to which the grading theorem applies.

<a id="stmt-prop-marked"></a>
<div id="prop:marked" class="proposition">

**Proposition 10** (Retaining the identity marking). *Fix a basic block order $R$ and a finite group $H$. Let $\mathcal A$ be a collection of $H$-crossed orders on $R$, each Morita equivalent to a finite-group block. If the total orders in $\mathcal A$ have finitely many $\mathcal O$-Morita classes, then $\mathcal A$ has finitely many based graded isomorphism classes.*

</div>

<details id="proof-stmt-prop-marked"><summary>Proof of stmt-prop-marked</summary>

*Proof.* *The total order.* Every order in the collection has rank $|H|\operatorname{rank}_{\mathcal O}R$. Choose a basic representative in each of its finitely many Morita classes. Any order in that class is an endomorphism order of a projective generator $\bigoplus_i P_i^{m_i}$, with positive integer multiplicities $m_i$ and the $P_i$ ranging over the finitely many indecomposable projectives. Its endomorphism order contains $M_{m_i}(\operatorname{End}(P_i))$ as a direct $\mathcal O$-module summand, so its rank bounds $m_i^2$. There are consequently only finitely many ungraded isomorphism types in the collection.

*The grading and the marking.* Lemma <a href="#lem:HH" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:HH">7</a> and Theorem <a href="#thm:gradings" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:gradings">8</a> give finitely many labelled $H$-gradings on every such total order. Fix one graded representative with identity component isomorphic to $R$. Two identifications of that component with $R$ differ by an element of $\operatorname{Aut}_{\mathcal O}(R)$. Inner changes extend to graded automorphisms of the total order by conjugation with a unit in degree one. Since $\operatorname{Out}_{\mathcal O}(R)$ is finite by Lemma <a href="#lem:HH" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="lem:HH">7</a>, there are finitely many remaining markings. These are precisely the based graded types in the assertion. ◻

</details>

<a id="sec:comparison"></a>
### Teichmüller overlaps and integral Sylow comparison

The rationality bound for a Sylow restriction must compare its multiplication factors as well as its identity block. We obtain it from the explicit operators in Section 9 of (OpenAI 2026a) <a id="pm-cite-26"></a>[[OpenAIFieldDonovan2026](#ref-OpenAIFieldDonovan2026)]. Proposition 9.1 there asserts exact equivariance after reduction; here we prove exact equivariance over $\mathcal O$ by following the scalar errors through the entire integral construction. Their membership in the Teichmüller group is the point that permits the final normalization.

<a id="pm-h-16"></a>
#### The scalar coefficient group

Write $$\mathcal T=[k^\times]\subseteq\mathcal O^\times$$ for the Teichmüller subgroup. Since $k$ is the algebraic closure of a finite field, $\mathcal T$ consists precisely of the roots of unity in $\mathcal O$ of order prime to $p$. It is preserved by Witt Frobenius, which acts on it by $p$th powering.
